What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I’ve been feeling uneasy around people. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people are actually watching you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: I… I can’t say for sure. But it feels that way to me. Like people at church or in the walking club are watching, and sometimes the bulletin or things they say seem like they’re meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages or watches are actually happening, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to prove it. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get that feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess… it could be. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling… like people at church or the walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things they say feel like messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages have changed over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly stayed the same, I think. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get that feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess… it could be. I don’t have proof. Maybe if there was a clear reason for it all… but even on better days, I still can’t really shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I’ve been feeling uneasy around people. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake the sense that some folks at church, and in my walking club, are watching me… maybe putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people are actually watching you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: I… I can’t say for sure. But it feels that way to me. Like people at church or in the walking club are watching, and sometimes the bulletin or things they say seem like they’re meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages or watches are actually happening, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to prove it. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling… like people at church or the walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things they say feel like messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages have changed over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: It’s… mostly stayed the same, I think. Even when I’m having a better day, I still get that feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess… it could be. I don’t have proof. But even on better days, I still can’t shake the feeling that people at church or the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in things they say or in the bulletin.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being watched or sent messages are definitely true, no matter what anyone else says?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say definitely, no. I know I can’t prove it. It’s just… even when I’m doing better, I still can’t shake that feeling that people at church or the walking club are watching me, or that little things are meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you did not believe people were watching you or sending you messages?\n\nPatient: Not really… even on my better days, it’s still there some. That feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that something in the bulletin or what they say is kind of meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your feelings of being watched or receiving hidden messages are based on reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe not fully. I came in because even when I’m having a better day, I still feel like some people at church or the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It feels real to me, even if I can’t prove it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything unusual about your thoughts or beliefs that might be different from how other people think?\n\nPatient: I suppose it is unusual, yes. Even if someone explained it, I think part of me would still feel like people at church or the walking club are watching me, or that little things said or printed are meant about me. It’s hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could convince you that these beliefs about being watched or sent messages are not true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Maybe if there was a clear reason for it all… but even on better days, I still can’t really shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It just… stays with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting the intentions of those around you, even when there's no clear reason to do so?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, I guess. Mostly with people at church and the walking club. Even when things are going all right, I still get this feeling they’re watching me, or that something in the bulletin or something someone says is meant for me. I know it may not look clear from the outside, but it’s hard for me to let go of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your feelings of being watched are not based on real events?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yes… but it doesn’t really settle it for me. Even on my better days, I can’t shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that little things in the bulletin or what they say are messages about me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your overall way of thinking and behaving has been pretty consistent throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I suppose I’ve mostly been the same person, but… I don’t know if I’d say everything has been consistent. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your way of thinking or behaving over the course of your life?\n\nPatient: I think it changed after my close friend died. Not right away maybe, but not long after. It’s been… about a year and five months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often suspect that others are out to harm you or are against you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say they’re out to harm me exactly. It’s more… I feel like some people at church, or in the walking club, are watching me. Like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say.
